The baseline reaches 915.7 at 105K; fixed $\\dt{=}2$ (006) peaks at 485.3 before collapsing to 132.9 ($14\\%$ of baseline); fixed $\\dt{=}4$ (007) never exceeds 176.9 ($19\\%$); the learned gate (005) collapses to 98.8 ($11\\%$).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.65\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_learning_curves.pdf}\n\\caption{Learning curves. The baseline (black) reaches 915.7; all variable-rate configurations degrade.}\n\\label{fig:learning_curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% F2: Trained dt_embedding → nve > 1.5x\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{F2: Trained Temporal Embedding Precedes Value Overestimation}\n\\label{sec:f2}\n\nThe factorial design isolates trained $\\dt$-embedding as the strongest predictor. The \\emph{primary} comparison---008d (trained, subsample ON) vs.\\ 008f (no embedding, subsample ON)---differs only in embedding training, yielding $\\nve = 4.12\\times$ (eval 82.8) vs.\\ $\\nve \\leq 0.97\\times$ (eval 503.0). \\emph{Secondary} comparisons against 008a/008c (unexposed embedding) co-vary subsample status and horizon alongside the embedding factor. The $\\nve \\geq 1.5\\times$ threshold (35K+ window) correctly classifies 26 of 29 experimental configurations across two environments (AUROC${=}0.953$, in-sample; LOEO cross-validated, Table~\\ref{tab:nve_loeo}; Figure~\\ref{fig:nve_trajectory}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.65\\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_nve_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{NVE trajectories (mean $\\pm$ seed range). Trained $\\dt$-embedding (red, Walker Walk, $n{=}3$ seeds plotted; seed~4 data in Table~\\ref{tab:complete_results}) exceeds the $1.5\\times$ threshold (dashed) in all seeds; no-embedding control (blue) remains near $1.0\\times$. Three trajectory types emerge: early collapse (seed~0), late-onset collapse (seed~3), and self-correction (seed~2). Dotted line marks the 35K diagnostic window onset.}\n\\label{fig:nve_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe trained $\\dt$-embedding precedes CIVO in all five Walker Walk seeds (peak NVE: $4.72\\times$, $4.62\\times$, $4.12\\times$, $1.62\\times$, $1.53\\times$), universally degrading performance to below 30\\% of baseline (IQM $175.2$, mean $170.6$, $n{=}5$; \\citealt{agarwal2021precipice}). Downstream trajectories diverge: two seeds undergo full collapse, one exhibits late-onset collapse still declining at 105K, and two self-correct (seeds 2 and 5). Combined with Cheetah Run (\\S\\ref{sec:f6}), CIVO onset is universal across all seven tested seeds ($n{=}7$, two environments) with 5/7 progressing toward collapse, 2/7 self-correcting (Appendix~\\ref{sec:seed_divergence}).\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% F3--F5: Summary (details in Appendix B)\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{F3--F5: Factorial Ablation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_factorial})}\n\\label{sec:f3f5_summary}\n\nTrained $\\dt$-embedding and multi-rate data exposure are \\emph{jointly sufficient in tested configurations} for CIVO. Neither factor alone precedes pathological overestimation: trained embedding without multi-rate exposure (008a, 3 seeds, peak NVE $0.83$--$0.93\\times$) and multi-rate exposure without embedding (008f, 3 seeds, peak NVE $\\leq 1.41\\times$) both remain below $1.5\\times$. Cross-environment replication on Cheetah Run confirms generalization. Freezing the embedding (\\texttt{requires\\_grad=False}) dramatically reduces severity (NVE $0.19$--$1.88\\times$ vs.\\ trained $1.53$--$4.72\\times$; 2/4 seeds below threshold, 2/4 transient self-correcting spikes; Table~\\ref{tab:three_way_emb}); removing the embedding (008f) also avoids CIVO. Partial gradient blocking (exp\\_044) does not prevent onset (peak NVE $4.45\\times$) but permits delayed self-correction (final eval 538, 59\\% baseline; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_factorial}), confirming that \\emph{any} gradient pathway updating $e_{\\dt}$ suffices to induce CIVO. Degradation scales monotonically with $\\dt$ ($-47\\%$ at $\\dt{=}2$, $-81\\%$ at $\\dt{=}4$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dt_amplification}).\n\n\\paragraph{ManiSkill manipulation tasks.}\nNone of three ManiSkill environments exhibit $\\dt$-embedding-dependent CIVO. StackCube-v1 value-ratio spikes are environment-specific: trained (049/052, NVE $5.05$/$4.67\\times$) and frozen (051, $4.30\\times$) match, all self-correcting by 82.5K. PegInsertionSide-v1 exhibits high-amplitude stochastic dynamics ($4$--$11\\times$) whose trained/frozen ordering is seed-dependent: seed~0 trained peaks higher ($10.05\\times$ vs.\\ frozen $1.82\\times$), but seed~1 reverses (frozen peak $11.32\\times$ vs.\\ trained peak $3.08\\times$). PickCube-v1 frozen control (exp\\_054, peak $8.81\\times$ at 62.5K, self-correcting) confirms the same pattern. CIVO is locomotion-specific in DreamerV3; the frozen-embedding diagnostic correctly identifies these transients as non-CIVO.\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% F6--F7: Interventions and cross-environment\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{F6--F7: Interventions and Multiple Pathways}\n\\label{sec:f6}\n\n\\paragraph{F6: Trained embedding creates multiple vulnerability pathways.}\nExp\\_008e\\_v3 restricts imagination to $\\dt{=}1$ while retaining the trained embedding. Two of four seeds collapse (50\\%): Pathway~A (CIVO, $\\nve = 4.02\\times$) and preliminary Pathway~B (entropy \\emph{increase}, $\\nve = 3.78\\times$; 2 seeds only, requires confirmation; Appendix~\\ref{sec:pathways}). Pathway~B produces one of three non-CIVO false positives ($n{=}29$).\n\n\\paragraph{F7: Full-pathway gradient stopping shows mixed results.}\nFull-SGS (exp\\_018) detaches all non-KL gradients from $e_{\\dt}$. Seed~1 shows partial mitigation (eval 448.3, NVE $1.66\\times$); seed~2 collapses (0/2 elimination; Appendix~\\ref{sec:sgs_gradient_analysis}). Cross-environment validation on Cheetah (010b, NVE $8.27$--$108.52\\times$) confirms CIVO generalization; full-SGS yields 0/3 elimination. Removing the embedding eliminates CIVO on Cheetah (024b, NVE $< 0.2\\times$).\n\n\\paragraph{Targeted obs-decoder-only SGS confirms pathway redundancy.}\nObs-decoder-only SGS (exp\\_025/026) achieves 1/6 CIVO prevention (1/4 Walker, 0/2 Cheetah; Table~\\ref{tab:intervention_hierarchy}), not meaningfully different from full-pathway SGS (0/3). CIVO operates through redundant gradient channels: blocking any single pathway shifts co-adaptation to the remaining ones (Appendix~\\ref{sec:seed_divergence}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Intervention hierarchy. Prevention = peak NVE $< 1.5\\times$ (35K+). No level reliably eliminates CIVO across environments.}\n\\label{tab:intervention_hierarchy}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Level} & \\textbf{Intervention} & \\textbf{Seeds} & \\textbf{Prev.} & \\textbf{Rate} \\\\\n\\midrule\n--- & Untreated & 7 & 0/7 & 0\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nGradient & Reward-only SGS & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\nGradient & Full-pathway SGS & 3 & 0/3 & 0\\% \\\\\nGradient & Obs-decoder SGS & 6 & 1/6 & 17\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nOutput & RAVE ($c{=}1.5{,}2.0$) & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}0.1$) & 1 & 1/1$^\\ddagger$ & 100\\% \\\\\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}0.2$) & 6$^\\dagger$ & 2/6 & 33\\% \\\\\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}0.5$) & 3 & 0/3 & 0\\% \\\\\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}1.0$) & 1 & 1/1$^\\S$ & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nArch. & Wide single & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\nArch. & Narrow dual (min) & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\nArch. & Wide dual (min) & 4 & 3/4$^\\|$ & 75\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n--- & Embedding frozen & 7 & 5/7$^\\#$ & 71\\% \\\\\n--- & Embedding removed & 5 & 5/5 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\raggedright\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$Walker 2/4 (s2 NVE $2.11\\times$; s3 late-onset CIVO, NVE $3.04\\times$ at 105K); Cheetah 0/1, PickCube 0/1.\n$^\\ddagger$Borderline (NVE${=}1.02\\times$, eval 55\\%).\n$^\\S$CIVO prevented but progressive policy erosion (peak 116\\%, final 15\\% baseline).\n$^\\|$Walker 3/3 ($\\nve \\leq 1.4\\times$; 1/3 non-CIVO eval collapse), Cheetah 0/1.\\\\\n$^\\#$Walker 5 + Cheetah 2. Exp\\_047 (NVE $1.71\\times$) and Exp\\_057 (NVE $1.88\\times$): both transient, self-correcting within 20K steps; remaining 5 seeds: NVE $0.19$--$1.45\\times$.\\par}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% Forward-pass interventions\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{Forward-Pass Interventions}\n\\label{sec:forward_results}\n\nWe test two forward-pass alternatives (Table~\\ref{tab:intervention_hierarchy}). \\textbf{RAVE} (value-level capping) fails: the EMA anchor tracks biased values during CIVO onset. \\textbf{TCN} (representation-level noise) suppresses CIVO in 2 of 4 Walker seeds at $\\sigma{=}0.2$ (NVE $< 1.0\\times$, eval 45--91\\%), but remaining seeds show runaway escalation, suggesting TCN gates rather than attenuates CIVO. Cross-environment transfer fails: Cheetah effective dose ($\\sigma{=}1.0$) causes policy erosion to 15\\% baseline; PickCube 0/1 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:tcn_cross_env}). TCN is a \\emph{diagnostic probe}, not a deployable mitigation.\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% Architecture-Level Ablation\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{Architecture-Level Ablation}\n\\label{sec:arch_results}\n\n\\paragraph{Critic convergence in pure imagination.}\nAcross all dual-critic configurations ($n{=}7$), critics trained on shared RSSM features converge ($\\rho > 0.999$), with pessimism at 3--4\\% of the independent-critic bound (\\S\\ref{sec:dual_critic_method}).\n\n\\paragraph{$2{\\times}2$ factorial: recovery vs.\\ prevention.}\nMin drives recovery: narrow dual-critic (${\\sim}1.2$M) recovers fully (${\\sim}16$--$19\\times$ recovery ratio) despite $2\\times$ fewer parameters. Prevention requires min $+$ capacity: wide dual-critic maintains $\\nve \\leq 1.4\\times$ (3/3 prevention, 1/3 non-CIVO collapse). Cross-environment transfer is limited (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dual_critic_appendix}).\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% Online CIVO Detection\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{Online CIVO Detection}\n\\label{sec:detector}\n\nWe derive a lightweight online detector monitoring the value-to-evaluation ratio for superlinear growth. Applied to 14 runs across three environments, it achieves TPR${=}5/5$, FPR${=}2/9$, with predictive alerts in 2/5 cases (Appendix~\\ref{sec:detector_appendix}).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:methodology}\n\n\\subsection{Base Architecture}\n\\label{sec:base_arch}\n\nAll experiments build on DreamerV3 \\citep{hafner2023dreamerv3}, which learns a recurrent state-space model (RSSM) with discrete latent states ($32$ categories $\\times$ $32$ classes), uses symlog-transformed predictions, and trains an actor-critic entirely within imagined trajectories. The standard configuration imagines $H{=}15$ steps at a fixed temporal resolution of $\\dt{=}1$, meaning each imagination step corresponds to one environment transition.\n\n\\subsection{Variable-Rate Imagination Mechanism}\n\\label{sec:variable_rate}\n\nWe introduce three orthogonal architectural modifications that enable variable-rate imagination in DreamerV3. Each can be independently toggled to isolate its contribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal embedding.}\nA learned embedding $e(\\dt)$ is concatenated to the RSSM input at each imagination step to condition state predictions on the temporal scale. We test three modes:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Trained}: embedding parameters are updated via backpropagation through the world model loss, so the RSSM can co-adapt its dynamics and reward predictions to the temporal scale.\n\\item \\textbf{Frozen}: a fixed random projection of identical dimensionality (\\texttt{requires\\_grad=False}). The RSSM receives a temporal signal but cannot co-adapt to it.\n\\item \\textbf{Removed}: embedding dimension set to zero; no temporal conditioning signal is provided.\n\\end{itemize}\nWe further distinguish \\textbf{trained-and-exposed} (subsample ON: the RSSM trains on $\\dt{>}1$ transitions) from \\textbf{trained-but-unexposed} (subsample OFF: $\\dt{>}1$ used only during imagination). This four-way classification (trained/exposed, trained/unexposed, frozen, removed) is the key experimental structure of our factorial design.\n\n\\paragraph{Temporal subsampling.}\nWhen enabled, the replay buffer augments training data with variable-$\\dt$ transitions by subsampling consecutive frames. Concretely, with a maximum subsampling factor of~2, the RSSM trains on both $\\dt{=}1$ and $\\dt{=}2$ transitions drawn from the same episodes. When disabled, only standard $\\dt{=}1$ transitions are used. This factor controls whether the world model has seen multi-scale dynamics during training.\n\n\\paragraph{Imagination horizon and step size.}\nThe imagination horizon $H$ sets the number of rollout steps. We test $H{=}15$ (default) and $H{=}8$. The reduced horizon controls for compounding error: $H{=}8$ steps at $\\dt{=}2$ covers $16$ physical time steps, comparable to the $15$ physical steps of the default configuration. The parameter \\texttt{gate\\_fixed\\_dt} forces all imagination steps to use a specific $\\dt$ value ($1$, $2$, or $4$); this separates the effect of $\\dt$-conditioning from any learned gating policy.\n\n\\subsection{Factorial Design}\n\\label{sec:factorial}\n\nWe adopt a factorial ablation across four factors: $\\dt$-embedding mode (trained / frozen / none), temporal subsampling (on / off), imagination horizon ($H{=}15$ / $H{=}8$), and imagination step size ($\\dt{=}1$, $2$, $4$). The design (12 conditions, Table~\\ref{tab:factorial_design} in Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_factorial}) isolates trained $\\dt$-embedding and multi-rate data exposure as orthogonal factors. Key configurations use 4 seeds; following \\citet{agarwal2021precipice}, we report per-seed trajectories.\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Metrics}\n\\label{sec:metrics}\n\n\\paragraph{Normalized value-to-eval ratio ($\\nve$).}\nWe define $\\nve_t = (v/e)_{\\text{config},t} / (v/e)_{\\text{baseline},t}$, where $v/e$ is the critic's mean imagined value divided by mean evaluation return, and the baseline is the highest-performing seed (exp\\_001, seed~1, 505K steps). We restrict to a \\textbf{35K+ window} to avoid early-training inflation. The $\\nve \\geq 1.5\\times$ threshold achieves 26/29 accuracy (AUROC${=}0.953$, in-sample; LOEO cross-validated, mean AUROC${=}0.981$, Appendix~Table~\\ref{tab:nve_loeo}).\n\n\\subsection{Selective Gradient Stopping}\n\\label{sec:sgs_method}\n\nTo identify which gradient pathway through $e_{\\Delta t}$ drives CIVO, we introduce \\textit{selective gradient stopping} (SGS): $e_{\\Delta t}$ is detached via $\\operatorname{sg}(\\cdot)$ before selected prediction heads during the backward pass, preventing those loss gradients from updating $e_{\\Delta t}$ while preserving its forward-pass contribution. We test three variants: reward-only (exp\\_016e), full-pathway (exp\\_018, all non-KL gradients blocked), and obs-decoder-only (exp\\_025/026). Gradient decomposition using $\\nabla_\\theta \\mathcal{L}_i$ quantifies each pathway's contribution (Appendix~\\ref{sec:sgs_gradient_analysis}).\n\n\\subsection{Forward-Pass Interventions}\n\\label{sec:forward_pass_method}\n\nWe test two forward-pass interventions as representation- and output-level probes: \\textbf{TCN} adds Gaussian noise $\\mathcal{N}(0, \\sigma^2)$ to $e_{\\Delta t}$ during imagination ($\\sigma \\in \\{0.5, 0.2\\}$, three environments); \\textbf{RAVE} clips imagined values exceeding an EMA anchor ($c \\in \\{2.0, 1.5\\}$).\n\n\\subsection{Dual-Critic Mechanism Ablation}\n\\label{sec:dual_critic_method}\n\nTo test whether standard overestimation countermeasures address CIVO, we conduct a $2{\\times}2$ factorial: \\{single, dual\\} $\\times$ \\{narrow (${\\sim}1.2$M), wide (${\\sim}2.4$M)\\} critic parameters. The dual-critic variant follows TD3 \\citep{fujimoto2018td3}: two independent value heads trained on shared RSSM features, with the actor optimizing $\\min(v_1, v_2)$. The wide variant doubles hidden dimensions to control for capacity independently of the min operation.\n\n\\subsection{Environment and Training Details}\n\\label{sec:env_details}\n\nAll experiments use Walker Walk and Cheetah Run from the DeepMind Control Suite (DMC; \\citealt{tassa2018dmcontrol}) with proprioceptive observations, chosen for their contrasting dynamics (6-joint biped vs.\\ 2-joint runner). Walker Walk serves as the primary environment with the full factorial design; Cheetah Run serves as a severity-dependent generalization probe: CIVO manifests at $8$--$70\\times$ NVE on Cheetah versus $2$--$4\\times$ on Walker, testing whether interventions transfer under amplified severity rather than replicating the full factorial. The baseline runs for 500K steps (peak return 970.4); treatments run for 105K steps, sufficient to observe collapse onset. All hyperparameters follow the DreamerV3 defaults \\citep{hafner2023dreamerv3} without task-specific tuning ($d{=}32$ temporal embedding; full hyperparameters in Appendix~\\ref{sec:hyperparameters}). Training uses a single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPU (${\\sim}80$ GPU-hours total). The codebase extends the official DreamerV3 implementation\\footnote{\\url{https://github.com/danijar/dreamerv3}, accessed March 2026.} with the variable-rate modules described in \\S\\ref{sec:variable_rate}.\n",

We restrict to a \\textbf{35K+ window} to avoid early-training inflation. The baseline reaches 915.7 at 105K; fixed $\\dt{=}2$ (006) peaks at 485.3 before collapsing to 132.9 ($14\\%$ of baseline); fixed $\\dt{=}4$ (007) never exceeds 176.9 ($19\\%$); the learned gate (005) collapses to 98.8 ($11\\%$).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.65\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_learning_curves.pdf}\n\\caption{Learning curves. The baseline (black) reaches 915.7; all variable-rate configurations degrade.}\n\\label{fig:learning_curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% F2: Trained dt_embedding → nve > 1.5x\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{F2: Trained Temporal Embedding Precedes Value Overestimation}\n\\label{sec:f2}\n\nThe factorial design isolates trained $\\dt$-embedding as the strongest predictor. The \\emph{primary} comparison---008d (trained, subsample ON) vs.\\ 008f (no embedding, subsample ON)---differs only in embedding training, yielding $\\nve = 4.12\\times$ (eval 82.8) vs.\\ $\\nve \\leq 0.97\\times$ (eval 503.0). \\emph{Secondary} comparisons against 008a/008c (unexposed embedding) co-vary subsample status and horizon alongside the embedding factor. The $\\nve \\geq 1.5\\times$ threshold (35K+ window) correctly classifies 26 of 29 experimental configurations across two environments (AUROC${=}0.953$, in-sample; LOEO cross-validated, Table~\\ref{tab:nve_loeo}; Figure~\\ref{fig:nve_trajectory}). %% TODO: RECALCULATE 26/29, AUROC after adding exp_059 (n=29→30)\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.65\\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_nve_trajectory.pdf}\n\\caption{NVE trajectories (mean $\\pm$ seed range). Trained $\\dt$-embedding (red, Walker Walk, $n{=}3$ seeds plotted; seed~4 data in Table~\\ref{tab:complete_results}) exceeds the $1.5\\times$ threshold (dashed) in all seeds; no-embedding control (blue) remains near $1.0\\times$. Three trajectory types emerge: early collapse (seed~0), late-onset collapse (seed~3), and self-correction (seed~2). Dotted line marks the 35K diagnostic window onset.}\n\\label{fig:nve_trajectory}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe trained $\\dt$-embedding precedes CIVO in all five Walker Walk seeds (peak NVE: $4.72\\times$, $4.62\\times$, $4.12\\times$, $1.62\\times$, $1.53\\times$), universally degrading performance to below 30\\% of baseline (IQM $175.2$, mean $170.6$, $n{=}5$; \\citealt{agarwal2021precipice}). Downstream trajectories diverge: two seeds undergo full collapse, one exhibits late-onset collapse still declining at 105K, and two self-correct (seeds 2 and 5). Combined with Cheetah Run (\\S\\ref{sec:f6}), CIVO onset is universal across all seven tested seeds ($n{=}7$, two environments) with 5/7 progressing toward collapse, 2/7 self-correcting (Appendix~\\ref{sec:seed_divergence}).\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% F3--F5: Summary (details in Appendix B)\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{F3--F5: Factorial Ablation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_factorial})}\n\\label{sec:f3f5_summary}\n\nTrained $\\dt$-embedding and multi-rate data exposure are \\emph{jointly sufficient in tested configurations} for CIVO. Neither factor alone precedes pathological overestimation: trained embedding without multi-rate exposure (008a, 3 seeds, peak NVE $0.83$--$0.93\\times$) and multi-rate exposure without embedding (008f, 3 seeds, peak NVE $\\leq 1.41\\times$) both remain below $1.5\\times$. Cross-environment replication on Cheetah Run confirms generalization. Freezing the embedding (\\texttt{requires\\_grad=False}) dramatically reduces severity (NVE $0.19$--$1.88\\times$ vs.\\ trained $1.53$--$4.72\\times$; 2/4 seeds below threshold, 2/4 transient self-correcting spikes; Table~\\ref{tab:three_way_emb}); removing the embedding (008f) also avoids CIVO. Partial gradient blocking (exp\\_044) does not prevent onset (peak NVE $4.45\\times$) but permits delayed self-correction (final eval 538, 59\\% baseline; Appendix~\\ref{sec:appendix_factorial}), confirming that \\emph{any} gradient pathway updating $e_{\\dt}$ suffices to induce CIVO. Degradation scales monotonically with $\\dt$ ($-47\\%$ at $\\dt{=}2$, $-81\\%$ at $\\dt{=}4$; Figure~\\ref{fig:dt_amplification}).\n\n\\paragraph{ManiSkill manipulation tasks.}\nNone of three ManiSkill environments exhibit $\\dt$-embedding-dependent CIVO. StackCube-v1 value-ratio spikes are environment-specific: trained (049/052, NVE $5.05$/$4.67\\times$) and frozen (051, $4.30\\times$) match, all self-correcting by 82.5K. PegInsertionSide-v1 exhibits high-amplitude stochastic dynamics ($4$--$11\\times$) whose trained/frozen ordering is seed-dependent: seed~0 trained peaks higher ($10.05\\times$ vs.\\ frozen $1.82\\times$), but seed~1 reverses (frozen peak $11.32\\times$ vs.\\ trained peak $3.08\\times$). PickCube-v1 frozen control (exp\\_054, peak $8.81\\times$ at 62.5K, self-correcting) confirms the same pattern. CIVO is locomotion-specific in DreamerV3; the frozen-embedding diagnostic correctly identifies these transients as non-CIVO.\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% F6--F7: Interventions and cross-environment\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{F6--F7: Interventions and Multiple Pathways}\n\\label{sec:f6}\n\n\\paragraph{F6: Trained embedding creates multiple vulnerability pathways.}\nExp\\_008e\\_v3 restricts imagination to $\\dt{=}1$ while retaining the trained embedding. Two of four seeds collapse (50\\%): Pathway~A (CIVO, $\\nve = 4.02\\times$) and preliminary Pathway~B (entropy \\emph{increase}, $\\nve = 3.78\\times$; 2 seeds only, requires confirmation; Appendix~\\ref{sec:pathways}). Pathway~B produces one of three non-CIVO false positives ($n{=}29$).\n\n\\paragraph{F7: Full-pathway gradient stopping shows mixed results.}\nFull-SGS (exp\\_018) detaches all non-KL gradients from $e_{\\dt}$. Seed~1 shows partial mitigation (eval 448.3, NVE $1.66\\times$); seed~2 collapses (0/2 elimination; Appendix~\\ref{sec:sgs_gradient_analysis}). Cross-environment validation on Cheetah (010b, NVE $8.27$--$108.52\\times$) confirms CIVO generalization; full-SGS yields 0/3 elimination. Removing the embedding eliminates CIVO on Cheetah (024b, NVE $< 0.2\\times$).\n\n\\paragraph{Targeted obs-decoder-only SGS confirms pathway redundancy.}\nObs-decoder-only SGS (exp\\_025/026) achieves 1/6 CIVO prevention (1/4 Walker, 0/2 Cheetah; Table~\\ref{tab:intervention_hierarchy}), not meaningfully different from full-pathway SGS (0/3). CIVO operates through redundant gradient channels: blocking any single pathway shifts co-adaptation to the remaining ones (Appendix~\\ref{sec:seed_divergence}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Intervention hierarchy. Prevention = peak NVE $< 1.5\\times$ (35K+). No level reliably eliminates CIVO across environments.}\n\\label{tab:intervention_hierarchy}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Level} & \\textbf{Intervention} & \\textbf{Seeds} & \\textbf{Prev.} & \\textbf{Rate} \\\\\n\\midrule\n--- & Untreated & 7 & 0/7 & 0\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nGradient & Reward-only SGS & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\nGradient & Full-pathway SGS & 3 & 0/3 & 0\\% \\\\\nGradient & Obs-decoder SGS & 6 & 1/6 & 17\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nOutput & RAVE ($c{=}1.5{,}2.0$) & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}0.1$) & 1 & 1/1$^\\ddagger$ & 100\\% \\\\\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}0.2$) & 6$^\\dagger$ & 2/6 & 33\\% \\\\\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}0.5$) & 3 & 0/3 & 0\\% \\\\\nRepr. & TCN ($\\sigma{=}1.0$) & 1 & 1/1$^\\S$ & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nArch. & Wide single & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\nArch. & Narrow dual (min) & 2 & 0/2 & 0\\% \\\\\nArch. & Wide dual (min) & 4 & 3/4$^\\|$ & 75\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n--- & Embedding frozen & 7 & 5/7$^\\#$ & 71\\% \\\\\n--- & Embedding removed & 5 & 5/5 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\raggedright\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$Walker 2/4 (s2 NVE $2.11\\times$; s3 late-onset CIVO, NVE $3.04\\times$ at 105K); Cheetah 0/1, PickCube 0/1.\n$^\\ddagger$Borderline (NVE${=}1.02\\times$, eval 55\\%).\n$^\\S$CIVO prevented but progressive policy erosion (peak 116\\%, final 15\\% baseline).\n$^\\|$Walker 3/3 ($\\nve \\leq 1.4\\times$; 1/3 non-CIVO eval collapse), Cheetah 0/1.\\\\\n$^\\#$Walker 5 + Cheetah 2. Exp\\_047 (NVE $1.71\\times$) and Exp\\_057 (NVE $1.88\\times$): both transient, self-correcting within 20K steps; remaining 5 seeds: NVE $0.19$--$1.45\\times$.\\par}\n\\end{table}\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% Forward-pass interventions\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{Forward-Pass Interventions}\n\\label{sec:forward_results}\n\nWe test two forward-pass alternatives (Table~\\ref{tab:intervention_hierarchy}). \\textbf{RAVE} (value-level capping) fails: the EMA anchor tracks biased values during CIVO onset. \\textbf{TCN} (representation-level noise) suppresses CIVO in 2 of 4 Walker seeds at $\\sigma{=}0.2$ (NVE $< 1.0\\times$, eval 45--91\\%), but remaining seeds show runaway escalation, suggesting TCN gates rather than attenuates CIVO. Cross-environment transfer fails: Cheetah effective dose ($\\sigma{=}1.0$) causes policy erosion to 15\\% baseline; PickCube 0/1 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:tcn_cross_env}). TCN is a \\emph{diagnostic probe}, not a deployable mitigation.\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% Architecture-Level Ablation\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{Architecture-Level Ablation}\n\\label{sec:arch_results}\n\n\\paragraph{Critic convergence in pure imagination.}\nAcross all dual-critic configurations ($n{=}7$), critics trained on shared RSSM features converge ($\\rho > 0.999$), with pessimism at 3--4\\% of the independent-critic bound (\\S\\ref{sec:dual_critic_method}).\n\n\\paragraph{$2{\\times}2$ factorial: recovery vs.\\ prevention.}\nMin drives recovery: narrow dual-critic (${\\sim}1.2$M) recovers fully (${\\sim}16$--$19\\times$ recovery ratio) despite $2\\times$ fewer parameters. Prevention requires min $+$ capacity: wide dual-critic maintains $\\nve \\leq 1.4\\times$ (3/3 prevention, 1/3 non-CIVO collapse). Cross-environment transfer is limited (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dual_critic_appendix}).\n\n%% ============================================================\n%% Online CIVO Detection\n%% ============================================================\n\n\\subsection{Online CIVO Detection}\n\\label{sec:detector}\n\nWe derive a lightweight online detector monitoring the value-to-evaluation ratio for superlinear growth. Applied to 14 runs across three environments, it achieves TPR${=}5/5$, FPR${=}2/9$, with predictive alerts in 2/5 cases (Appendix~\\ref{sec:detector_appendix}).\n",

271	Single-seed normalization (s1) yields AUROC $= 0.933$ and accuracy 7/8 (87.5\%) on the Walker subset. Four-seed mean normalization yields AUROC $= 0.867$ and accuracy 6/8 (75.0\%): the boundary case exp\_009b flips from $\nve = 1.62\times$ (s1) to $1.23\times$ (4-seed mean), because its peak occurs at 45K when the s1 $v/e$ ratio (0.331) is well below the 4-seed mean (0.436). The $1.5\times$ threshold thus correctly identifies all strong CIVO cases regardless of baseline choice; sensitivity is confined to configurations within 10\% of the threshold.
